## 2 Related W<sub>o</sub>rk

Autonomous research benchmarks. PaperBench (Starace et al., 2025) evaluates replication of published research, while EXP-Bench (Kon et al., 2026) assesses the desi<sub>g</sub>n and execution of paperd<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s emp</sub>h<sub>as</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>ti<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>researc</sub>h content. More open-ended settin<sub>g</sub>s include MLE-bench (Chan et al., 2025) and RE-Bench (Wijk et al., 2025), which evaluate ML en<sub>g</sub>ineerin<sub>g</sub> and research, alon<sub>g</sub>side MLG<sub>y</sub>m (Nathani et al., 2025), MLRC-Bench (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025c), and AstaBench (Bra<sub>gg</sub> et al., 2026), which support standardized ex<sub>p</sub>erimentation and e<sub>v</sub>aluation across ML and scientific tasks. Within lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e modelin<sub>g,</sub> Kar<sub>p</sub>ath<sub>y</sub>’s autoresearch (Karpath<sub>y</sub>, 2026) minimizes validation loss under a fixed trainin<sub>g</sub>-time bud<sub>g</sub>et, whereas NanoGPT Bench (Intolo<sub>gy</sub>, 2026) tar<sub>g</sub>ets faster trainin<sub>g</sub> at a required model qualit<sub>y</sub>. Related benchmarks evaluate reproduction of NanoGPT improvements (Zhao et al., 2025) and correctnessconstrained computational speedups (Ou<sub>y</sub>an<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Press et al., 2025; Shett<sub>y</sub> et al., 2025). H<sub>oweve</sub>r<sub>, e</sub>xi<sub>s</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> b<sub>e</sub>n<sub>c</sub>hm<sub>a</sub>rk<sub>s p</sub>r<sub>ov</sub>id<sub>e</sub> limit<sub>e</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>o</sub>f <sub>au</sub>t<sub>o</sub>-r<sub>esea</sub>r<sub>c</sub>h <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>s ac</sub>r<sub>oss co</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub>- mentary objectives. Arena instead uni<sup>fi</sup>es diverse objectives wit<sup>h</sup>in a s<sup>h</sup>ared experimenta<sup>l</sup> setting, revea<sup>l</sup>ing <sup>h</sup>ow t<sup>h</sup>e researc<sup>h</sup> objective s<sup>h</sup>apes bot<sup>h h</sup>arness per<sup>f</sup>ormance and t<sup>h</sup>e resu<sup>l</sup>ting idea <sup>f</sup>rontier.

Autonomous research systems. Complementing these benchmarks, research harnesses determine h<sub>ow age</sub>nt<sub>s o</sub>r<sub>ga</sub>niz<sub>e e</sub>x<sub>pe</sub>rim<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>s a</sub>nd <sub>use accu</sub>m<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>e</sub>n<sub>ce.</sub> End-t<sub>o</sub>-<sub>e</sub>nd <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>s co</sub>nn<sub>ec</sub>t ideation, experimentation, and reportin<sub>g</sub> (Lu et al., 2026; Tan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025), while AIDE (Jian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025) and AIRA (Toledo et al., 2025) or<sub>g</sub>anize iterative code improvement throu<sub>g</sub>h search. AutoScientists (Gao et al., 2026) coordinates decentralized teams, Arbor (Jin et al., 2026) maintains a persistent h<sub>y</sub>pothesis tree, and GEAR (Jeddi et al., 2026) evolves populations of research states. Heuresis (Antoniades et al., 2026) further compares search strate<sub>g</sub>ies across qualit<sub>y</sub>, diversit<sub>y</sub>, and nove<sup>l</sup>ty. Arena eva<sup>l</sup>uates <sup>h</sup>ow t<sup>h</sup>e re<sup>l</sup>ative strengt<sup>h</sup>s o<sup>f</sup> researc<sup>h</sup> <sup>h</sup>arnesses vary across objectives.

## <sub>3.</sub>2 L<sub>anguage-</sub>M<sub>odeling</sub> I<sub>nstantiation</sub>

We instantiate Arena usin<sub>g</sub> Karpath<sub>y</sub>’s autoresearch lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-modelin<sub>g</sub> testbed (Karpath<sub>y</sub>, 2026), in which candidate solutions are com<sub>p</sub>act LLM <sub>p</sub>retrainin<sub>g p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>rams. We retain its fixed trainin<sub>g</sub>-data <sub>p</sub>ool and li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht ex<sub>p</sub>erimental setu<sub>p,</sub> re<sub>p</sub>lacin<sub>g</sub> loss minimization with the constrained formulation in Eq. 1. Eac<sup>h</sup> trac<sup>k</sup> optimizes one o<sup>f</sup> nine e<sup>fi</sup>ciency metrics subject to requirements on mode<sup>l</sup> <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> and relevant non-tar<sub>g</sub>et resources.

Model quality. Following the original testbed (Karpathy, 2026), we measure language-modeling qualit<sub>y</sub> usin<sub>g</sub> validation bits per b<sub>y</sub>te (BPB). A candidate is admissible onl<sub>y</sub> if its validation BPB is below 1.05. This threshold is slightly above the reference program’s BPB, providing flexibility for structural chan<sub>g</sub>es while limitin<sub>g q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> de<sub>g</sub>radation.

Resource constraints. Each track limits relevant non-target resources to control the trade-ofs permitted durin<sub>g</sub> o<sub>p</sub>timization. These constraints cover <sub>q</sub>uantities such as model size<sub>,</sub> trainin<sub>g</sub> FLOPs<sub>,</sub> <sub>p</sub>eak trainin<sub>g</sub> memor<sub>y,</sub> and available trainin<sub>g</sub> data. Measurement. The Arena evaluator measures all target metrics and constrained resources directly. Reference baselines. We introduce two simple reference harnesses that difer primarily in how many research directions they retain. Sequential Search maintains one program and evaluates one modification at a time, adopting an admissible improvement before continuing. Beam Search retains <sub>up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>our programs an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es</sub> t<sub>wo mo</sub>difi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ons o</sub>f <sub>eac</sub>h b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e nex</sub>t f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er.</sub> B<sub>o</sub>th <sub>co</sub>nditi<sub>o</sub>n <sub>su</sub>b<sub>seque</sub>nt <sub>p</sub>r<sub>oposa</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>o</sub>n th<sub>e</sub> <sub>accu</sub>m<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>e</sub>x<sub>pe</sub>rim<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>l hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r<sub>y,</sub> in<sub>c</sub>l<sub>u</sub>din<sub>g</sub> <sub>u</sub>n<sub>success</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l attem<sub>p</sub>ts. Tasks and resources. We evaluate all nine Arena objectives defined in Sec. 3, each with targetspecific resource constraints and the shared quality requirement BPB < 1.05. Models and harnesses. We evaluate six LLM-based research harnesses with Claude Opus 5 (Anthropic, 2026) and GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, 2026), both usin<sub>g</sub> medium reasonin<sub>g</sub> efort. In addition to Sequential Search and Beam Search, we evaluate AutoScientists (Gao et al., 2026), Arbor (Jin et al., 2026), GEAR (Jeddi et al., 2026), and Heuresis (Antoniades et al., 2026), which respectivel<sub>y</sub> use multi-a<sub>g</sub>ent coordination<sub>,</sub> h<sub>yp</sub>othesis trees<sub>,</sub> evolutionar<sub>y</sub> search<sub>,</sub> and diversit<sub>y</sub>-<sub>p</sub>reservin<sub>g</sub> archives. We additionall<sub>y</sub> include TPE (Ferreira et al., 2026), a non-a<sub>g</sub>entic baseline restricted to a predefined <sup>h</sup>yperparameter space, in t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>O</sup>pus <sup>5</sup> experiments.

Scoring. Each objective is scored by the best recorded admissible target value, with lower values better. For trainin<sub>g</sub>-token consum<sub>p</sub>tion<sub>,</sub> o<sub>p</sub>timizer u<sub>p</sub>dates<sub>,</sub> and trainin<sub>g</sub> time<sub>,</sub> we use the first 30- second validation checkpoint satisfying BPB < 1.05. We summarize performance across objectives with diferent units and scales usin<sub>g</sub> Elo ratin<sub>g</sub>s from <sub>p</sub>airwise harness com<sub>p</sub>arisons within each objective. Appendix D gives t<sup>h</sup>e scoring procedure.

Arena provides a discriminative evaluation. Research harnesses exhibit substantial diferences across Arena’s objectives, wit<sup>h</sup> no sing<sup>l</sup>e met<sup>h</sup>od dominating t<sup>h</sup>e benc<sup>h</sup>mar<sup>k</sup>. Wit<sup>h</sup> Opus 5, Auto-S<sub>c</sub>i<sub>e</sub>nti<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t <sub>agg</sub>r<sub>ega</sub>t<sub>e</sub> El<sub>o,</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> S<sub>eque</sub>nti<sub>a</sub>l S<sub>ea</sub>r<sub>c</sub>h <sub>a</sub>nd Arb<sub>o</sub>r<sub>; w</sub>ith GPT-6<sub>,</sub> GEAR ran<sup>k</sup>s <sup>fi</sup>rst, <sup>f</sup>o<sup>ll</sup>owed by Sequentia<sup>l</sup> Searc<sup>h</sup>. T<sup>h</sup>e variation across objectives s<sup>h</sup>ows t<sup>h</sup>at Arena distin<sub>g</sub>uishes com<sub>p</sub>lementar<sub>y</sub> stren<sub>g</sub>ths of research s<sub>y</sub>stems rather than reducin<sub>g</sub> their <sub>p</sub>erformance to a sin<sub>g</sub>le research settin<sub>g</sub>.

Simple search is surprisingly competitive. How we measure it. The model’s total parameter count after the fixed 600-second training schedule.

Long-prompt latency (request latency)

Why it matters. Lower request latency reduces the full delay of processing a long context and generating a <sup>res</sup>p<sup>onse.</sup>

How we measure it. Changes optimization while retaining the architectural backbone.</td></tr><tr><td>Square-root cooldown</td><td>Heuresis</td><td>Training time</td><td>Changes the learning-rate schedule during later training.</td></tr><tr><td>Per-head attention out- put gate</td><td>Sequential Search</td><td>Training time</td><td>Introduces learned control over individual attention-head outputs.</td></tr><tr><td>Sparse value-residual gate channels</td><td>AutoScientists</td><td>Parameters</td><td>Restricts value-residual gating to a subset of channels.</td></tr><tr><td>Shorter attention win- dow</td><td>GEAR</td><td>Training updates</td><td>Changes the amount of context processed by attention.</td></tr><tr><td>Layer-specific MLP widths</td><td>AutoScientists</td><td>Parameters</td><td>Redistributes MLP capacity across depth while approximately preserving total width.</td></tr><tr><td>Value embeddings at se- Sequential Search lected stack depths</td><td></td><td>Training time</td><td>Moves value-residual injection layers at depth {1,3,5,7} to {0,1,6,7}.</td></tr><tr><td>Two independent half- Heuresis width MLP sublayers</td><td></td><td>Training tokens</td><td>Motivates a final design with two indepen- dent MLP updates within each block.</td></tr></table>

The mar<sub>g</sub>in multi<sub>p</sub>lier $m _ { a , b , j }$ <sub>g</sub>rows <sub>g</sub>raduall<sub>y</sub> with the mar<sub>g</sub>in and is ca<sub>pp</sub>ed at 3 so that one <sub>u</sub>n<sub>usua</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> l<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub> <sub>w</sub>in <sub>ca</sub>nn<sub>o</sub>t d<sub>o</sub>min<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> r<sub>a</sub>nkin<sub>g.</sub> Ex<sub>ac</sub>t ti<sub>es</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> <sub>a</sub> z<sub>e</sub>r<sub>o</sub> m<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>li<sub>e</sub>r <sub>a</sub>nd <sub>cause</sub> n<sub>o</sub> rating up<sup>d</sup>ate.

Weight objectives and update both ratings

## E<sub>.</sub>1 A<sub>utonomous-</sub>R<sub>esearch</sub> B<sub>enchmarks</sub>

Implementation, reproduction, and research tasks. Benchmarks cover repository execution, method implementation, reproduction, and expert-specified extensions (Bo<sub>g</sub>in et al., 2024; Sie<sub>g</sub>el <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2025<sub>;</sub> H<sub>ua</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2025<sub>;</sub> Y<sub>an</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2025<sub>;</sub> St<sub>arace</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2025<sub>;</sub> K<sub>on</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2026<sub>;</sub> Ed<sub>war</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.,</sub> 2026). Related capabilit<sub>y</sub> suites assess software and command-line tasks (Jimenez et al., 2024; Merrill et al., 2026), retrieval (Wei et al., 2025; Xion<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026), scientific discover<sub>y</sub> and pro<sub>g</sub>rammin<sub>g</sub> (Majumder et al., 2025; Chen et al., 2025b; Tian et al., 2024), and theorem provin<sub>g</sub> (Tsoukalas et al., 2024). Arena instead leaves the improvement mechanism open and evaluates a measured optimization tar<sub>g</sub>et under <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> and resource constraints.

Open-ended research and research artifacts. Machine-learning engineering, research competitions<sub>,</sub> and <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er-derived research environments su<sub>pp</sub>ort o<sub>p</sub>en-ended ex<sub>p</sub>erimentation under taskspecific evaluation protocols (Chan et al., 2025; Miller et al., 2025; Wijk et al., 2025; Nathani et al., 2025; Garikaparthi et al., 2026; Lupidi et al., 2026; Wu et al., 2026; Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025c). Other evaluations examine <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osals<sub>,</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>eriments<sub>,</sub> re<sub>p</sub>orts<sub>,</sub> ali<sub>g</sub>nment<sub>,</sub> and artifact su<sub>pp</sub>ort across research sta<sub>g</sub>es (Tan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Liu et al., 2026; Chen et al., 2025a; Xu et al., 2026; Cui et al., 2026; Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026; Bra<sub>gg</sub> et al., 2026). Arena focuses on how harness performance chan<sub>g</sub>es across objectives in one <sup>l</sup>anguage-mode<sup>l</sup>ing environment, ran<sup>k</sup>ing measured endpoints and using traces as ex<sub>p</sub>lanator<sub>y</sub> evidence.

Language-model and computational optimization. Language-model benchmarks evaluate validation loss<sub>,</sub> trainin<sub>g</sub> s<sub>p</sub>eed<sub>,</sub> re<sub>p</sub>roduction of historical im<sub>p</sub>rovements<sub>,</sub> or <sub>p</sub>ost-trainin<sub>g</sub> under compute constraints (Karpath<sub>y</sub>, 2026; Intolo<sub>gy</sub>, 2026; Zhao et al., 2025; Rank et al., 2026); s<sub>y</sub>stems b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t k<sub>erne</sub>l<sub>s, numer</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>rou</sub>ti<sub>nes, an</sub>d <sub>so</sub>ft<sub>ware per</sub>f<sub>ormance un</sub>d<sub>er correc</sub>t<sub>ness requ</sub>i<sub>re-</sub> ments (Ou<sub>y</sub>an<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Press et al., 2025; Shett<sub>y</sub> et al., 2025). Arena spans nine model, trainin<sub>g</sub>, and in<sup>f</sup>erence objectives wit<sup>h</sup> a common qua<sup>l</sup>ity requirement and objective-speci<sup>fi</sup>c resource <sup>l</sup>imits.

Evaluation validity and reliability. Studies examine eficiency and scafold contributions after benchmark saturation (Nad<sub>g</sub>ir et al., 2026), specification and evaluator inconsistencies (Tu et al., 2026), and hardware sensitivit<sub>y</sub> and score a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation (Chen et al., 2026). These concerns motivate distin<sub>g</sub>uishin<sub>g</sub> valid resource im<sub>p</sub>rovements from a<sub>pp</sub>arent <sub>g</sub>ains caused b<sub>y</sub> evaluation artifacts.

## E<sub>.</sub>2 A<sub>utonomous-</sub>R<sub>esearch</sub> H<sub>arnesses</sub>

End-to-end workflows. Research systems connect literature review, idea generation, implementation, experiments, anal<sub>y</sub>sis, and reportin<sub>g</sub> (Lu et al., 2026; Tan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Trirat et al., 2025; Schmid all et al., 2025). The research harness orchestrates these activities around a foundation <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

Tree, archive, and evolutionary search. Harnesses allocate experiments through code trees, hypothesis trees, archives, and alternative selection policies (Jian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Toledo et al., 2025; Jin et al., 2026; Antoniades et al., 2026). Population-based methods retain competin<sub>g</sub> pro<sub>g</sub>rams or heuristics and <sub>g</sub>enerate candidates usin<sub>g</sub> executable feedback, mutation, or crossover (Romera Paredes et al., 2024; Liu et al., 2024; Novikov et al., 2025; Jeddi et al., 2026). These search procedures or<sub>g</sub>anize ex<sub>p</sub>loration<sub>; p</sub>erformance <sub>g</sub>ains alone do not establish scientific novelt<sub>y</sub>.

Multi-agent coordination and persistent memory. Teams, proposal critique, execution recovery, and shared successful or failed experiments support sustained research (Gao et al., 2026; Liu et al., 2026). Semantic, episodic, and structured scientific memor<sub>y</sub> provide complementar<sub>y</sub> mechanisms for retainin<sub>g</sub> knowled<sub>g</sub>e across sessions (Fan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Gottweis et al., 2026; Mitchener et al., 2025).

Harness design and self-improvement. Other methods optimize agent architectures, workflows, or implementations throu<sub>g</sub>h meta-search and recursive improvement (Hu et al., 2025; Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025b;a; Srikanth et al., 2026). Their search object is the research procedure itself, rather than only the candidate ex<sub>p</sub>erimental <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>ram.

Arena eva<sup>l</sup>uates <sup>h</sup>arness–mode<sup>l</sup> combinations across mu<sup>l</sup>tip<sup>l</sup>e optimization objectives in a s<sup>h</sup>ared lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-modelin<sub>g</sub> environment<sub>,</sub> leavin<sub>g</sub> the search strate<sub>gy</sub> o<sub>p</sub>en and retainin<sub>g</sub> standardized ex<sub>p</sub>eri<sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l t<sub>races.</sub>
